L2074: Reviewer Comment: The applicant notes that the influence of EDTA (vs. heparin) may reflect competition between the drug and anticoagulant for metal chelation, which is not an uncommon phenomenon for tetracyclines. Measuring serum concentrations of TP-434 may avoid potentially confounding effects of anticoagulants that work via metal chelation.
L2075: REVIEWER ASSESSMENT: Data from this experiment suggests that eravacycline is not preferentially taken up by or bound to blood cells. The B:P ratio was increased with increasing concentrations of TP-434 in this study only when EDTA or sodium citrate was present in the test system, which may reflect competition for metal ion chelation.

L2077: *In Vitro* Protein Binding of Eravacycline (TP-434) in Human Plasma, Serum Albumin and α1-Acid Glycoprotein
L2078: Date(s): June 2015 to July 2015
L2079: Sponsor: TetraPhase Pharmaceuticals, Inc. Watertown, MA
L2080: Testing Facility:

L2082: ## METHODS
L2083: The *in vitro* protein binding of 0.1, 0.5, 1.0, 5.0 and 10 µg/mL eravacycline was determined in pooled, mixed-gender human plasma and in solutions of human serum albumin (HSA) and human α1-acid glycoprotein (AGP) after dialysis for 4 h at 37°C under 5% CO2 with protection from light. Concentrations of eravacycline were determined by LC/MS/MS. Propranolol and warfarin were used as positive controls for the protein binding study.
L2084: ## RESULTS
L2085: The protein binding of eravacycline in human plasma was concentration-dependent with binding increasing with increasing drug concentration (Table 1). Protein binding of eravacycline to human AGP was also concentration-dependent; however, in AGP solution, drug binding
L2086: ## NDA Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 211109
## XERAVA (eravacycline) for injection
L2087: decreased with increasing eravacycline concentration (Table 2). In HSA solution, the protein binding of eravacycline was consistent over the concentration range examined (Table 3).
L2088: For the positive control propranolol (1.0 µg/mL), the mean free fractions were 29.2, 57.2 and 8.3% in plasma, HSA solution and AGP solution, respectively. Warfarin (1.0 µg/mL) was highly bound in all matrices with mean free fractions of 1.0% in plasma, 0.6% in HSA solution and 2.7% in AGP solution.

L2105: **Reviewer Comment:** At the highest concentration of eravacycline tested (10 \mug/mL) the unbound fraction of drug decreased in human plasma (fu=13.1%), increased in AGP solution (fu=58.6%) and remained relatively unchanged in HSA solution (fu=36.1%). The Sponsor does not include a rationale for this phenomenon; however, its relevance is uncertain given that concentrations in vivo are significantly lower than 10 \mug/mL.
L2106: **REVIEWER ASSESSMENT:** The protein binding of eravacycline in human plasma was concentration-dependent with binding increasing with increasing drug concentration. The mean free fractions of eravacycline in human plasma were 31.6 \pm 1.4, 26.7 \pm 3.1, 24.6 \pm 1.0, 23.0 \pm 1.0 and 13.1 \pm 6.4% at 0.1, 0.5, 1.0, 5.0 and 10 \mug/mL eravacycline, respectively.
L2107: **Study No.:** [REDACTED](b) (4)-001
L2108: The protein binding of eravacycline (TP-434) in plasma from rabbit, mouse, rat, human, cynomolgus monkey and African green monkey determined by in vitro microdialysis
L2109: Date(s): May 2016
L2110: Sponsor: TetraPhase Pharmaceuticals, Inc. Watertown, MA
L2111: Testing Facility: [REDACTED](b) (4)
L2112: ## METHODS
L2113: Plasma protein binding was studied in pooled (minimum of six animals) plasma from different species (rabbit, mouse, rat, human, cynomolgus monkey, and African green monkey) at four concentrations of eravacycline (0.1, 1, 10 and 100 \mug/mL) in the absence and presence of the metabolite TP- 6208 using *in vitro* microdialysis. A sensitive HPLC-MS/MS method was used to quantify eravacycline concentrations in saline and plasma. The percent free fraction was calculated using Equation 1, where C_{\text{Dialysate}} (T1, T2) is the dialysate collected between time interval T1 and T2, CPRT (T1) and CPRT (T2) are the concentrations of eravacycline in plasma at
L2114: time T1 and T2, respectively. The calculated percent free fractions were averaged to calculate final percent free fraction.
L2115: Equation 1.
L2116: \% \text{ Free Fraction} = \frac{C_{\text{Dialysate (T1,T2)}}}{C_{\text{P}_{\text{RT}}(\text{T1})} + C_{\text{P}_{\text{RT}}(\text{T2})}} \times \frac{100}{\% \text{ Probe Recovery}} \times 100
L2117: ## RESULTS
L2118: Eravacycline showed concentration-dependent plasma protein binding in all species over the range of 0.1-100 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}. The effect of a lower concentration of TP-6208 (0.1 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}) on the plasma protein binding of eravacycline was investigated because 1 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} TP-6208 significantly altered the free fraction of eravacycline in human plasma. In contrast to the results obtained with 1 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}, TP-6208 (0.1 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}) did not consistently decrease the plasma binding of eravacycline at concentrations between 0.1 and 3 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} in human plasma (Table 1).
L2119: Table 1. Plasma protein binding of eravacycline (mean \pm SD) in pooled plasma in the presence and absence of TP-6208 (1\mu\text{g}/\text{mL}) determined by microdialysis: Repeat experiment

Eravacycline +

L2121: Reviewer Comment: The steady state total C_{\text{max}} of eravacycline is 1.825 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} as determined in previous studies. Data from this study projects the percent free drug in plasma at these concentrations to be approximately 1-5%. The percent free fraction of eravacycline observed between different lots of pooled human plasma was associated with significant variability in this study. The applicant notes that the reasons for such variability are unknown. There is also notable inter-study variability (up to 5-fold), which may be due to low affinity binding at lower concentrations, variability in pooled plasma sources or inter-laboratory variability in the methods used (equilibrium dialysis, MD, ultrafiltration).
L2122: REVIEWER ASSESSMENT: The percentage of free eravacycline in human plasma decreased with increasing concentrations, and was highly variable between experiments.
L2123: Study No. (b) (4) 085084
L2124: ## In Vitro Evaluation of TP-434 as an Inhibitor of Human Cytochrome P450 Enzymes
L2125: Date(s): December 1, 2008 to January 15, 2009

L2128: ## METHODS
L2129: To evaluate TP-434 (eravacycline) as a direct inhibitor of CYP activity, pooled human liver microsomes (CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6 and CYP3A4/5) from 16 individuals were incubated with marker substrates, at concentrations approximately equal to their apparent K_m, in the presence or absence of TP-434. L2134: ## RESULTS
L2135: For the direct inhibition assays, positive control inhibited the enzyme activity in all cases. For time-dependent assays, positive control inhibited the enzyme activity in a metabolism-dependent manner for all additional zero-minute and 30 minute preincubations. Test article interference and test article suppression check samples performed as expected indicating that the addition of TP-434 had little or no impact on the validated analytical methods used in this study. Additionally, metabolite formation was directly proportional to protein concentration and incubation time in all cases. Results of the evaluation of TP-434 as a direct and time-dependent inhibitor of human CYP enzymes are summarized in **Table 2**.

L2139: **REVIEWER ASSESSMENT:** In this study, there was little or no evidence that TP-434 caused direct inhibition of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, or CYP3A4/5 and the IC50 values for these enzymes were reported to be greater than the highest concentration of TP-434 evaluated (i.e., 85.6 \muM). There was little or no evidence of time-dependent inhibition of any of the CYP enzymes evaluated by TP-434 as no distinct increase in inhibition was observed upon preincubation.
L2140: Despite evidence of direct inhibition of CYP2C8 by TP-434 (~45% enzyme inhibition at the highest concentration of TP-434 of 85.6 \muM) *in vitro*, is unlikely to be clinically relevant at steady-state Cmax concentrations of TP-434 produced by a 1.0 mg/kg i.v. dose given every 12 h (1.8 \muM)..

L2206: ## Induction of CYP1A2, 2B6, and 3A4 Enzyme Activities in Fresh Human Hepatocytes

L2210: ## **METHODS**
L2211: The *in vitro* induction effects of TP-434-046 (eravacycline; 0.1, 1, and 10 µM) on CYP1A2, 2B6, and 3A4 enzyme activities were evaluated using fresh human hepatocytes from three donors. Positive controls were treated in parallel with the induction media spiked with β-naphthoflavone at 10 µM for CYP1A2, phenobarbital at 1000 µM for CYP2B6, or rifampicin at 50 µM for CYP3A4. Vehicle controls were treated in parallel with the induction media containing 0.1% DMSO.

L2228: ## *In Vitro* Evaluation of TP-034 as an Inducer of Cytochrome P450 Expression in Cultured Human Hepatocytes
L2229: Date(s): July 30, 2015 to September 15, 2015
Sponsor: TetraPhase Pharmaceuticals, Inc. Watertown, MA
Testing Facility: (b)(4)
L2230: ## METHODS
L2231: Three preparations of cryopreserved human hepatocytes from three separate donors were treated once daily for three consecutive days with dimethyl sulfoxide (DMSO, 0.1% v/v, vehicle control), flumazenil (25 µM, negative control), one of eight concentrations of TP-034 (eravacycline; 0.01, 0.1, 0.3, 1, 3, 7.5, 10 or 30 µM) or one of three known human CYP inducers [omeprazole (50 µM), phenobarbital (750 µM) and rifampin (20 µM)]. The highest concentration of TP-034 tested was 120× plasma C_{max,ss} of TP-034 (0.25 µM) following intravenous dosing to steady state with the highest clinical doses of eravacycline. After treatment, the cells were incubated with the appropriate marker substrates for the analysis of phenacetin O-dealkylation (marker for CYP1A2), bupropion hydroxylation (marker for CYP2B6) and midazolam 1'-hydroxylation (marker for CYP3A4/5) by LC/MS/MS.
L2232: ## RESULTS
L2233: At the time of isolation the viability of each hepatocyte preparation was between 81.7 and 91%. Treatment of hepatocytes with TP-034 caused little or no LDH release (≤ 4.5%), relative to the positive control.
L2234: The enzyme induction studies with TP-034 at concentration ≥ 120 plasma C_{max,ss} suggest that TP-034 has no potential to cause clinically relevant induction of CYP1A2. However, based on estimates of E_{max} and EC_{50} for induction of CYP2B6 and CYP3A4 mRNA levels by TP-034, the value of R3 fell below 0.9 in two and three cultures of human hepatocytes, respectively (Figures 1 and 2). These results suggest TP-034 may have the potential to cause clinically relevant induction of CYP2B6 and CYP3A4.
L2235: *Reviewer Comment: The potential for clinically relevant induction based on the value of R3 was calculated according to the following equation, where d is a scaling factor set to 1 for the basic model and [I] is total (bound+unbound) plasma concentration at steady state, which is 0.15 µM for TP-034. Values of R3 less than 0.9 were considered potentially clinically relevant induction based on the FDA 2012 Guidance on Drug-drug Interactions.*
L2236: R_3 = \frac{1}{1 + d \left( \frac{E_{\max} \cdot [I]}{EC_{50} + [I]} \right)} &lt; 0.9
L2237: Figure 1. Estimates of E_{\max}, EC_{50} and R_3 for induction of CYP2B6 mRNA in cultured human hepatocytes treated with TP-034
L2238: a The slope was manually set to 1
L2239: § Calculations are based on a plasma C_{\max,ss} of 0.16 \muM (for oral dosing)
L2240: † Calculations are based on a plasma C_{\max,ss} of 0.25 \muM (for intravenous dosing)
L2241: Reviewer Comment: TP-034 increased CYP2B6 mRNA levels more than 20% of positive control in HC7-8 (63.6%) and less than 20% in HC10-10 (8.02%). In both cultures of human hepatocytes the values of R_3 were slightly below the FDA cutoff of 0.9. While these results may suggest TP-034 to cause induction of CYP2B6, it is not likely to be clinically significant
L2242: Figure 2. Estimates of E_{\max}, EC_{50} and R_3 for induction of CYP3A4 mRNA in cultured human hepatocytes treated with TP-034

L2254: ## *In Vitro* Evaluation of TP-6208 as an Inducer of Cytochrome P450 Expression in Cultured Human Hepatocytes
L2255: Date(s): June 18, 2013 to July 30, 2013
L2256: Sponsor: TetraPhase Pharmaceuticals, Inc. Watertown, MA
L2257: Testing Facility: (b) (4)
L2258: ## METHODS
L2259: Three preparations of cryopreserved human hepatocytes from three separate donors were treated once daily for three consecutive days with dimethyl sulfoxide (DMSO, 0.1% v/v, vehicle control), flumazenil (25 µM, negative control), one of six concentrations of TP-6208 (0.01, 0.1, 1, 3, 10 or 30 µM) or one of three known human CYP inducers [omeprazole (50 µM), phenobarbital (750 µM) and rifampin (20 µM)]. After treatment, the cells were incubated with the appropriate marker substrates for the analysis of phenacetin O-dealkylation (marker for CYP1A2), bupropion hydroxylation (marker for CYP2B6) and midazolam 1'-hydroxylation (marker for CYP3A4/5) by LC/MS/MS.
L2260: ## RESULTS
L2261: At the time of isolation the viability of each hepatocyte preparation was between 77 and 85%. Treatment of cultured human hepatocytes with up to 30 µM TP-6208 resulted in little or no
L2262: LDH release (&lt;20% of the positive LDH control) at 24, 48 or 72-hour time points. Positive controls caused anticipated and appropriate increases in CYP enzyme expression.
L2263: Overall, treatment of cultured human hepatocytes with up to 30 µM TP-6208 had little or no effect on CYP1A2 activity (range of 0.690- to 1.07-fold), on CYP2B6 activity (range of 0.775- to 1.85-fold) or on CYP3A4/5 activity (range of 0.816- to 1.49-fold).
L2264: Similar to the negligible effect on activity, treatment of cultured human hepatocytes with up to 30 µM TP-6208 produced little or no increase of CYP1A2 mRNA (range of 0.865- to 1.70-fold), CYP2B6 mRNA (range of 0.908- to 1.57-fold) or CYP3A4 mRNA levels (range 0.760- to 1.51-fold).
L2265: **REVIEWER ASSESSMENT:** Treatment of hepatocytes with up to 30 µM TP-6208 produced little or no increase (&lt; 2-fold) in CYP1A2, CYP2B6, or CYP3A4 mRNA and enzyme activity levels.
L2266: Study No (b) (4)143046
L2267: ## *In Vitro* Evaluation of Eravacycline and TP-498 as Inducers of Cytochrome P450 Expression in Cultured Human Hepatocytes
L2268: Date(s): August 27, 2014 to October 6, 2014
L2269: Sponsor: TetraPhase Pharmaceuticals, Inc. Watertown, MA
L2270: Testing Facility: (b) (4)
L2271: ## METHODS
L2272: Three cryopreserved preparations of cultured human hepatocytes from three separate livers were treated once daily for three consecutive days with dimethyl sulfoxide flumazenil (negative control), one of eight concentrations of TP-498 (0.04, 0.2, 2, 4, 10, 20, 60 or 100 µM), one of thirteen concentrations of eravacycline (0.06, 0.1, 0.3, 0.6, 1, 2, 3, 6, 10, 30, 60, 100 or 200 µM) or one of three known human CYP inducers [omeprazole (50 µM), phenobarbital (750 µM) and rifampin (20 µM) as positive controls]. After treatment, the cells were incubated with the appropriate marker substrates for the analysis of phenacetin *O*-dealkylation (marker for CYP1A2), bupropion hydroxylation (marker for CYP2B6) and midazolam 1'-hydroxylation (marker for CYP3A4/5) by LC-MS/MS.
L2273: ## RESULTS
L2274: Cultured human hepatocytes treated with TP-498 at concentrations of 20 µM or above exhibited mild cytotoxicity. Treatment of cultured human hepatocytes with up to 3 µM eravacycline did not cause any detectable cytotoxicity, while those treated with eravacycline at concentrations of 6 µM exhibited mild cytotoxicity and those treated with concentrations of 100 µM or above exhibited severe cytotoxicity.
L2275: Treatment of cultured human hepatocytes with TP-498 at concentrations of up to 4 to 10 µM or with eravacycline at concentrations of up to 3 µM (concentrations that did not alter culture morphology and were not associated with cytotoxicity) did not induce CYP activity or mRNA
L2276: levels (\le 2.0-fold change or \le 10\% of positive control response) for CYP1A2, CYP2B6 and CYP3A4.
L2277: **REVIEWER ASSESSMENT:** CYP1A2, CYP2B6 and CYP3A4 were not induced with TP-498 at concentrations of up to 4 to 10 \muM or with eravacycline at concentrations of up to 3 \muM. The reason for the marked decreases in CYP activity and mRNA levels after incubation with high concentrations of TP-498 or eravacycline was not investigated. The Sponsor suggests that these changes in CYP activity and mRNA levels may be secondary to sublethal cytotoxicity observed at these concentrations.
L2278: STUDY NO.: TP-434-016
L2279: ## **A Phase 1, Open-Label Clinical Study to Assess the Impact of Itraconazole on Eravacycline Pharmacokinetics in Healthy Subjects**
L2280: Date(s): May 26, 2015 to October 13 2015
L2281: Sponsor: Tetraphase Pharmaceuticals, Inc. Watertown, MA
L2282: Clinical Site: Medpace CPU, Cincinnati, Ohio
L2283: (b) (4)
L2284: Analytical Site:
L2285: ## **METHODS**
L2286: This was a Phase 1, prospective, single-center, open-label clinical study to assess the PK interactions of itraconazole with eravacycline in healthy subjects. In Part A, subjects received a single 1.0 mg/kg dose of IV eravacycline on Day 1 as a 60-minute infusion, after breakfast and again on Day 10. Subjects received itraconazole 200 mg po every 12 hours on Day 8 and then every 24 hours from Day 9 to Day 10. Intravenous eravacycline was stopped after the second dosing on the morning of Day 10. Itraconazole was stopped after dosing on the morning of Day 10.
L2287: **PK Sample Collection:** Blood samples for PK analysis of eravacycline and metabolites were collected on Day 1 and Day 10 pre-dose and up to 48 hours after the start of the IV infusion.
L2288: **Drug Products:** Eravacycline for IV administration was supplied as a sterile injectable lyophilized powder in a 10 mL vial. Eravacycline drug product contains (b) (4) mg (TP-434 free base equivalents) of lyophilized powder and mannitol as an inactive (b) (4) Itraconazole was supplied as a 10mg/ml oral solution (Lot # EKB3D00).
L2289: **Analytical Methods:** Concentrations of eravacycline and metabolites, TP-498, TP-6208, and TP-034 in human plasma samples were measured by LC-MS/MS. The performance of the bioanalytical assays is acceptable.
L2290: ## **RESULTS**
L2291: The results of PK parameters of eravacycline and its metabolites (TP-498, TP-6208, and TP-034) on Day 1 and Day 10 are summarized in Tables 1 and 2.

L2303: REVIEWER ASSESSMENT: Following administration with itraconazole on Day 10, mean eravacycline AUC0-t increased approximately 32%. Similarly, there was a reduction in mean eravacycline clearance of approximately 32% and in mean AUC of its metabolites, TP-6208 and TP-034, of approximately 65%.
L2304: STUDY NO.: TP-434-020
L2305: ## **A Phase 1, Open-Label Clinical Study to Assess the Impact of Rifampin on Eravacycline Pharmacokinetics in Healthy Subjects**
L2306: Date(s): August 3, 2015 to October 21, 2015
L2307: Sponsor: Tetraphase Pharmaceuticals, Inc. Watertown, MA
L2308: Clinical Site: Medpace CPU, Cincinnati, Ohio
L2309: Analytical Site: (b) (4)
L2310: ## **METHODS**
L2311: This was a Phase 1, prospective, single-center, open-label clinical study to assess the PK interactions of oral rifampin with eravacycline in healthy subjects.
L2312: The treatment period consisted of 19 days, including a washout period. Subjects received single 1.0 mg/kg dose of IV eravacycline on Day 1 as a 60-minute infusion and again on Day 17. Subjects received rifampin 600 mg po every 24 hours from Day 8 to Day 17. Study drugs were administered under fasting conditions.
L2313: **PK Sample Collection:** Blood samples for PK analysis of eravacycline and metabolites were collected on Day 1 and Day 17 pre-dose and up to 48 hours after the start of the IV infusion.
L2314: **Drug Products:** Eravacycline for IV administration was supplied as a sterile injectable lyophilized powder in a 10 mL vial. Eravacycline drug product contains [REDACTED](b)(4) mg (TP-434 free base equivalents) of lyophilized powder and mannitol as an inactive [REDACTED](b)(4). Rifampin was supplied as a 600 mg capsule (Lot # ME130564).
L2315: **Analytical Methods:** Concentrations of eravacycline and metabolites, TP-498, TP-6208, and TP-034 in human plasma samples were measured by LC-MS/MS. The performance of the bioanalytical assays is acceptable.
L2316: ## RESULTS
L2317: Following coadministration with rifampin, the mean C_{max} of eravacycline was unchanged, while the mean AUC0-24h was decreased approximately 25%, mean CL increased approximately 54%, and the apparent half-life for eravacycline decreased approximately 27%. The results of PK parameters of eravacycline and its metabolites (TP-498, TP-6208, and TP-034) on Day 1 and Day 17 are summarized in Tables 1 and 2.

L2327: **REVIEWER ASSESSMENT:** Concomitant administration of eravacycline and rifampin to healthy subjects resulted in a decrease in AUC0-inf by 35%, an increase in CL 54% and a decrease in half-life of 27%, with negligible effects on Vss (13% decrease) and Cmax (8% increase).
L2328: ## TP-434-P1-SAD-1: Randomized, Placebo-controlled, Double-blind Study to Evaluate the Safety and Pharmacokinetics of Single Ascending Doses of TP-434
L2329: Date(s): 10/4/2009 – 11/27/2009
L2330: Sponsor: Tetraphase Pharmaceuticals, Inc., Watertown, MA
L2331: Clinical Site: Cetero Research, Fargo, ND
L2332: ## METHODS
L2333: **Study Design:** Study TP-434-P1-SAD-1 was a single-center, double-blind, placebo-controlled, randomized, single-ascending-dose study to assess the safety, tolerability, and PK of IV TP-434 (eravacycline, ERV) in 56 healthy male and female subjects. Subjects were randomized to receive 0.10 mg/kg, 0.25 mg/kg, 0.50 mg/kg, 1.00 mg/kg, 1.50 mg/kg, 2.00 mg/kg, or 3.00 mg/kg IV ERV or placebo over 30 minutes on Day 1 (active:placebo = 3:1). Safety was monitored over 9 days. 16 plasma and 6 urine samples were collected over 5 days.
L2334: ERV was the drug product and was supplied as lyophilized powder for reconstitution with sterile water prior to IV administration.
L2335: **Analytical Methods:** Bioanalytical assays TSLS09-137 and TSLS09-136 were used to measure ERV concentrations in plasma and urine, respectively. The performance was satisfactory.
L2336: ## NDA Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 211109

L2349: ## NDA Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 211109
## XERAVA (eravacycline) for injection
L2350: Reviewer Comment: The mean extrapolation ratio (AUC_{inf}/AUC_{0-t}) for ERV AUC_{inf} exceeds 1.2 in subjects in the 0.10 mg/kg cohort. The mean extrapolation ratio for TP-498 AUC_{inf} exceeds 1.2 in subjects in the 0.5 and 1.0 mg/kg cohorts. Therefore, the PK profiles in the respective dose cohorts were not adequately characterized. However, the PK profile for 1 mg/kg ERV, the proposed dose, was appropriately captured.

L2359: ## Dose Proportionality
L2360: As shown in Table 159 and Table 163, ERV AUC increased proportionally with increasing doses over the dose range of 0.25 to 3 mg/kg. C_{max} increased in a slightly greater than dose proportional fashion over the dose range of 0.25 to 3 mg/kg.

L2364: ## Safety:
L2365: The most common adverse events were nausea and vomiting, which had a higher incidence in higher dose cohorts (2 and 3 mg/kg). Nausea was reported in 3 (50%) subjects administered 2 mg/kg ERV and in 5 (83.3%) subjects administered 3 mg/kg ERV. Vomiting was reported in 3 (50%) subjects administered 3 mg/kg ERV. Neither nausea nor vomiting was reported in any subject taking ERV doses less than 2 mg/kg.
L2366: *Reviewer Comment: Nausea and vomiting appear to be dose-related.*
L2367: **REVIEWER ASSESSMENT:** ERV was well-tolerated at IV doses up to 1.5 mg/kg. ERV AUC increased proportionally, and Cmax increased slightly greater than dose proportionally with increasing doses over the dose range of 0.25 to 3 mg/kg. Based on the low fraction (&lt;20%) of administered dose excreted in urine, it appears that ERV is primarily nonrenally eliminated. Nausea and vomiting appear to be dose-limiting toxicities associated with IV doses greater than 1.5 mg/kg.
L2368: ## TP-434-P1-MAD-1: Randomized, Placebo-controlled, Double-blind Study to Evaluate the Safety and Pharmacokinetics of Multiple Ascending Dose Regimens of TP-434
L2369: Date(s): 11/8/2009 – 2/12/2010
Sponsor: Tetraphase Pharmaceuticals, Inc., Watertown, MA
Clinical Site: Cetero Research, Fargo, ND
L2370: ## METHODS
L2371: **Study Design:** Study TP-434-P1-MAD-1 was a single-center, double-blind, placebo-controlled, randomized, multiple-ascending-dose study to assess the safety, tolerability, and PK of IV TP-434 (eravacycline, ERV) in healthy subjects. Subjects were randomized to receive 0.50 mg/kg infused over 30 minutes QD, 1.50 mg/kg infused over 30 minutes QD, 1.50 mg/kg infused over 60 minutes QD, or 1.00 mg/kg infused over 60 minutes BID ERV or placebo for 10 days
L2372: (active:placebo = 6:2). Safety was monitored over 24 days. 36 or 37 plasma samples (one additional sample in the 1.00 mg BID cohort) and 12 urine samples were collected over 14 days.
L2373: ERV was the drug product and was supplied as lyophilized powder for reconstitution with sterile water prior to IV administration.
L2374: **Analytical Methods:** Bioanalytical assays (b) (4) 09-137 and (b) (4) 09-136 were used to measured ERV concentrations in plasma and urine, respectively. The performance was satisfactory.

L2390: Reviewer Comment: The t1/2 for the 1.00 mg/kg Q12 group is based on the AUC0-t instead of the AUCinf. The accumulation ratio for AUC, R(AUC), appears to consistently show accumulation of around 35% regardless of dose.
L2391: As shown in Table 165, eravacycline AUC0-24 and Cmax are roughly dose proportional between 0.5 mg/kg QD and 1.5 mg/kg QD.

L2400: *Reviewer Comment: It appears that the urine excretion of ERV increases over time. However, the urine collection interval on Day 1 may have been too short to capture all ERV excreted in the urine. Similarly, the ERV collected in the urine on Day 10 may have been from doses before Day 10 although the calculation of Fe(0-96) assumes that all of the excreted drug came from one dose.*
L2401: ## Safety:
L2402: The most common adverse events were related to infusion site conditions and nausea. Nausea appeared to show a relationship with dose. Nausea was reported once in the placebo cohort, 0 times in the 0.5 mg/kg cohort, 3 times in 2(33%) subjects in the 1.5 mg/kg over 30 minutes cohort, 9 times in 5 (83%) subjects in the 1.5 mg/kg over 60 minutes cohort, and 7 times in 4(67%) subjects in the 1.00 mg/kg BID cohort. Vomiting was only reported in one subject each in the 1.50 mg/kg over 30 minutes cohort and the 1.00 mg/kg cohort.
L2403: **REVIEWER ASSESSMENT:** The PK profile was not adequately assessed on Day 1 in the 1.00 mg/kg BID cohort. It appears that ERV has a long distribution phase. This may explain why the V increased from Day 1 to Day 10. This disagrees with the finding in the SAD study. However, the result in this study is more reflective of steady-state dosing.
L2404: Nausea appears to be the most common side effect of ERV based on the results from this study and the SAD study.
L2405: ## **TP-434-013: A Phase 1, Open-Label Study to Assess the Single-Dose Pharmacokinetics of Eravacycline in Subjects with Impaired Hepatic Function and Healthy Subjects**
L2406: Date(s): 10/2/2013 – 2/21/2014
Sponsor: Tetraphase Pharmaceuticals, Inc., Watertown, MA
Clinical Site: 3 sites in the US
L2407: ## **METHODS**
L2408: **Study Design:** Study TP-434-013 was a multicenter, open-label study to assess the safety, tolerability, and PK of a single dose of 1.5 mg/kg IV TP-434 (eravacycline, ERV) in healthy subjects and subjects with mild, moderate, and severe hepatic impairment per the Child-Pugh scale. 11 blood samples for PK analysis each of ERV, TP-498, and TP-6208 were collected.
L2409: **Analytical Methods:** Bioanalytical assay(b) (4) 12262 was used to measured ERV, TP-6208, and TP-498 concentrations in plasma. The performance was generally satisfactory with a few concerns. The calibration curve range was 5-500 ng/mL. However, the Cmax and other points in the concentration-time profile were above 500 ng/mL. Dilution quality control samples were assayed at 2500 ng/mL (above all measured concentrations), but only 2 times (2/24/14 and 2/26/14) out of 8 analytical runs from 12/11/13 to 3/13/14.
L2410: In addition, the validation reported listed a long-term stability of 54 days at -20C to -70C, but the study started on 10/2/13, and the first batch of samples was not assayed until 12/13/13, approximately 72 days later. Some of these samples were reanalyzed on 2/24/14, over 120 days from the study start date. However, other validation methods for ERV list long-term stability up to 3 months at -70C and 12 months at -80C.

L2419: **REVIEWER ASSESSMENT:** Results of this study show that eravacycline AUC increased with the severity of hepatic function, with 2-fold higher AUC in patients with severe hepatic impairment relative to healthy volunteers. However, the plasma exposure of eravacycline in the healthy volunteer cohort is significantly lower than seen in other Phase 1 trials.
L2420: ## **TP-434-014: A Phase 1, Open-Label Study to Assess the Single-Dose Pharmacokinetics of Eravacycline in Subjects with End Stage Renal Disease and Healthy Subjects**
L2421: Date(s): 1/8/2014 – 3/3/2014
L2422: Sponsor: Tetraphase Pharmaceuticals, Inc., Watertown, MA
L2423: Clinical Site: 2 sites in the US
L2424: ## **METHODS**
L2425: **Study Design:** Study TP-434-014 was a multicenter, open-label study to assess the safety, tolerability, and PK of a single dose of 1.5 mg/kg IV TP-434 (eravacycline, ERV) in healthy and subjects and subjects with end stage renal disease (ESRD). 11 blood samples for PK analysis each of ERV, TP-498, and TP-6208 were collected. For subjects receiving dialysis, ERV was
L2426: administered on a day when dialysis was not administered, and at least 8 samples of each analyte were collected within 24 hours of dosing.
L2427: ERV was the drug product. It was supplied as lyophilized powder for reconstitution with sterile water prior to IV administration.
L2428: **Analytical Methods:** Bioanalytical assay (b)(4)-12262 was used to measured ERV, TP-6208, and TP-498 concentrations in plasma. The performance was satisfactory.

L2435: **Protein Binding:** In Study (b)(4)-006, the protein binding in samples from subjects in the present study, TP-434-014, was analyzed. However, out of 72 samples, 65 samples had free concentrations of ERV that were below the limit of quantification, 10 ng/mL. The frequency of BLQ samples did not appear to correlate with the concentration in plasma.
L2436: **REVIEWER ASSESSMENT:** Results of this study showed no change in ERV AUC, C_{max}, or T_{1/2} between healthy subjects and subjects with ESRD. This agrees with the fact that ERV appears to be primarily metabolized. However, the plasma PK in the healthy volunteer cohort is significantly lower than seen in other Phase 1 trials. In addition, the protein binding substudy is inconclusive because most of the samples were below the limit of quantification.
L2437: ## **TP-434-012: An Open-Label, Single Dose Study Designed to Assess the Mass Balance Recovery and Metabolite Substudy (TET-P2989), Metabolite Profile and Identification of Metabolite Structure for [^{14}C]-Eravacycline in Healthy Male Subjects after Oral and Intravenous Dosing**
L2438: Date(s): 7/9/2014 – 8/4/2014
Sponsor: Tetrphase Pharmaceuticals, Inc., Watertown, MA
Clinical site: Quotient Clinical, Nottingham NG11 6JS, UK
L2439: ## **METHODS**
L2440: **Study Design:** Study TP-434-012 was a single center, open-label study to assess the mass balance recovery of a single dose of 100 mg oral TP-434 (eravacycline, ERV) and 60 mg IV ERV in healthy male subjects. 26 plasma samples for analysis of total radioactivity (TR), ERV, TP-498, and TP-6208 were collected. 26 whole blood samples for analysis of TR were collected. 9 plasma samples for metabolite identification were collected. 14 urine samples were collected for analysis of TR, ERV, TP-498, TP-6208, and metabolite identification. 12 samples of feces were collected for analysis of TR and metabolite identification.
L2441: *Reviewer Comment: Only the mass balance for IV ERV was reviewed because this NDA only covers IV ERV.*
L2442: **Drug Product and Dose:** 60 mg [^{14}C]-eravacycline was administered as a single IV dose by a 60-minute infusion containing NMT 3.8 MBq (105 \muCi) ^{14}C.
L2443: **Analytical Methods:** An LC-MS/MS assay (b) (4) 266344QB03) was used to measure ERV, TP-6208, and TP-498 concentrations in plasma. A separate HPLC-MS/MS bioanalytical assay was developed for the metabolite substudy (TET-P2989). However, the assay for TET-P2989 was not able to discriminate between TP-498 and TP-6208. Radioactivity was determined using a liquid scintillation counter. Overall, the performance of each bioanalytical assay was acceptable.
L2444: ## **RESULTS**
L2445: **Study Population:** In total, 5 subjects enrolled and completed the IV ERV arm of the study. There were no major clinical pharmacology-related protocol violations.

- Epimerization (Eravacycline \to TP-498)
- Oxidation (Eravacycline \to TP-6208)
- Oxidation (Eravacycline \to TP-034)
- Glucuronidation (Eravacycline \to TP-034)
- Oxidation (Eravacycline \to M572a, M572b)
- Oxidation (Eravacycline \to M590a, M590b)
- Amide hydrolysis (Eravacycline \to TP-034)

L2487: EDWARD M COX

L2488: Figure 2 consists of two scatter plots, A and B, showing the relationship between exposure and microbiological response at the Test-of-Cure (TOC) visit. Both plots show microbiological response (Y-axis, 0.0 to 1.0) versus exposure (X-axis). Plot A uses Day 1 Free AUC:MIC (log scale, 0.1 to 100.0) on the X-axis. Plot B uses Day 1 Free AUC (mcg*hr/L, 0.3 to 10.0) on the X-axis. Data points are represented by triangles, and a fitted curve is shown. A shaded area indicates the confidence interval. The fitted curve is relatively flat, suggesting a plateau effect, particularly for the exposures achieved in the Phase 2/3 studies.
L2489: Bar chart showing the relationship between Nausea/Vomiting (%) and AUC. The Y-axis is Nausea/Vomiting (%), ranging from 0 to 25. The X-axis is AUC, categorized as < 4.40 and >= 4.40. For AUC < 4.40, the incidence is 2.8% (2/72). For AUC >= 4.40, the incidence is 9.3% (50/537). The overall P-value is 0.06.
L2490: Figure 4 is a box plot illustrating the simulated Total Daily AUC (mg*hr/mL) of Eravacycline across five hepatic function groups (Normal, Mild HI, Moderate HI, Severe HI, and Severe HI with a modified dose regimen) over 14 days (Day 1, 4, 9, 14). The Y-axis represents Daily AUC (mg*hr/mL), ranging from 0 to 60. The X-axis represents Day.
The legend indicates the hepatic function groups:
Normal (Red)
Mild HI (Green)
Moderate HI (Teal)
Severe HI (Purple)
The plot shows that AUC generally increases with hepatic impairment severity, especially for the Severe HI group, where the AUC is significantly higher than the other groups, particularly on Day 14.
Reference lines are included:
Black dashed lines represent the 25th and 75th percentile of predicted AUC0-24h in patients with normal hepatic function administered 1 mg/kg q12h.
Blue dashed line represents the 75th percentile of predicted AUC0-24h in patients with mild hepatic impairment administered 1 mg/kg q12h.

L2492: Figure 6 displays six Goodness-of-Fit (GoF) plots for TETR-PCS-100, arranged in a 3x2 grid. The plots assess the model fit by comparing observed data (DV) against predicted values (PRED or IPRED) and residuals (CWRES or |IWRES|).
Top Left Plot: DV vs PRED. Shows a strong positive correlation, with data points generally following the diagonal line (red line).
Top Right Plot: DV vs IPRED. Shows a strong positive correlation, with data points generally following the diagonal line (red line).
Middle Left Plot: CWRES vs TIME. Residuals are scattered randomly around the horizontal line at 0, indicating no systematic bias.
Middle Right Plot: CWRES vs TAD. Residuals are scattered randomly around the horizontal line at 0, indicating no systematic bias.
Bottom Left Plot: CWRES vs PRED. Residuals are scattered randomly around the horizontal line at 0, indicating no systematic bias.
Bottom Right Plot: |IWRES| vs IPRED. Shows a positive correlation between the absolute value of the weighted residual and the predicted value, with data points generally following the diagonal line (red line).

L2494: Figure 8 displays four goodness-of-fit plots for the Uni Model, arranged in a 2x2 grid:
Top Left: Individual predictions versus concentrations. The plot shows Observed concentration (Y-axis, 0 to 15000) versus Individual predicted concentration (X-axis, 0 to 15000). Data points are scattered around the diagonal line (Observed = Predicted), indicating a good fit for individual predictions.
Top Right: Population predictions versus concentrations. The plot shows Observed concentration (Y-axis, 0 to 15000) versus Population predicted concentration (X-axis, 0 to 15000). Data points are scattered around the diagonal line, indicating a good fit for population predictions.
Bottom Left: Conditional weighted residuals versus population predictions. The plot shows CWRES (Conditional Weighted Residuals, Y-axis, -2 to 5) versus Population predicted concentration (X-axis, 0 to 8000). The residuals are randomly scattered around the horizontal line at CWRES = 0, suggesting no systematic bias in the population predictions.
Bottom Right: Conditional weighted residuals versus time. The plot shows CWRES (Y-axis, -2 to 5) versus Time after dose (X-axis, 0 to 600). The residuals are randomly scattered around the horizontal line at CWRES = 0, suggesting no systematic bias related to time.
L2495: Figure 9 displays three scatter plots comparing UniIPRED estimates against estimates from three different models (TETR-PCS-100, ICPD 00380-1, and ICPD 0381), along with the corresponding coefficient of determination (R^2).
A) TETR-PCS-100 (PCS-100): UniIPRED vs PCS-100 IPRED. The data points show a strong linear correlation, resulting in an R^2 value of 99.96%.
B) ICPD 00380-1: UniIPRED vs ICPD 00380-1 IPRED. The data points show a strong linear correlation, resulting in an R^2 value of 97.52%.
C) ICPD 0381: UniIPRED vs ICPD 0381 IPRED. The data points show a strong linear correlation, resulting in an R^2 value of 97.19%.
L2496: Figure 10 displays two scatter plots showing the relationship between exposure metrics and Microbiological Response (Y-axis, ranging from 0.0 to 1.0) at the Test-of-Cure Visit.
Plot A shows Microbiological Response versus Day 1 FAUC:MIC (X-axis, logarithmic scale from 0.1 to 100.0). The data points (triangles) are clustered near the top of the Y-axis (Microbiological Response close to 1.0), indicating high response across the measured exposure range.
Plot B shows Microbiological Response versus Day 1 Free AUC (mcg*hr/L) (X-axis, logarithmic scale from 0.3 to 10.0). The data points (triangles) are also clustered near the top of the Y-axis (Microbiological Response close to 1.0), indicating high response across the measured exposure range.
Both plots include shaded areas representing confidence intervals or uncertainty bounds, and a dashed horizontal line near Y=0.9.

1: [0.17, 3.29)
2: [3.29, 6.03)
3: [6.03, 12.06)
4: [12.06, 302.12)
The curves are very close together, indicating no significant difference in time to fever resolution across the quartiles.
